HYPERGO / HYPERZOD REFERENCE
30 September 2026

Scope and status
This describes the current local HyperGo source, the site-specific Hyperzod behavior observed during the project, and the failures recorded in the dated reference notes. It is a source and evidence reference, not a claim that every local change is published or verified in the installed iOS app. The 29 September customer follow-up was documented as local and unpublished at the time of its INSTALL note. Its Global file exactly matches the current PASTE_INTO_HYPERZOD Global file. The earlier Global/Web package was reported as published and loading on the customer website. Live publication status after those notes is not established here.

Authoritative local files:
PASTE_INTO_HYPERZOD/1_GLOBAL/GLOBAL__Custom_HTML_Footer.html
PASTE_INTO_HYPERZOD/2_WEB/WEB__Custom_HTML_Footer.html
PASTE_INTO_HYPERZOD/3_APP/APP__Custom_HTML_Footer.html
LANDING_PAGE/HyperGo_Landing_FULL_Refined_Icons.html
LANDING_PAGE/HyperGo_Landing_FULL_Refined_Icons.css

The two convenience extracts, PASTE_INTO_HYPERZOD/hypergo.css and hypergo.js, contain only the literal contents of style and script tags. They are not a substitute for the three complete footer fields. In particular, Global JavaScript creates more CSS at runtime; that CSS is in hypergo.js as string-building code, not in hypergo.css. Concatenating Global, Web and App JavaScript into one file also loses their intended placement and execution scope.

Code boxes
Hyperzod exposes Global, Web and App customization surfaces. Web has Custom HTML Head, Custom HTML Footer and Custom CSS; App has the corresponding Head, Footer and Custom CSS areas. Theme Builder also has section-level Custom CSS, including Header and Categories. The actual native category editor exists and is preferable for changing category artwork; the source artwork target was square, about 512 x 512 pixels. A separate landing HTML/CSS source exists, but the public root was observed redirecting to the marketplace. The exact current admin placement of that landing section was not established, so the landing files must be edited in their original section rather than guessed into a footer.

The current working delivery uses complete HTML snippets in the footer boxes: style rules inside <style> tags and JavaScript inside <script> tags. For a full replacement, replace the entire relevant field; appending a new copy below old code creates competing selectors, observers, event handlers and intervals. Plain CSS and plain JavaScript extracted into separate files are reference assets, not proven paste-ready content for an HTML Footer. Nothing in the evidence establishes that Hyperzod accepts an externally hosted .css or .js URL, a separate JavaScript box, a particular character limit, or a maximum number of tags. Do not invent such limits. The current local Global file is about 364 KB, Web about 9 KB and App about 4 KB; local parsing of those files does not prove any unpublished version has been accepted by the admin box.

Global is the shared owner of Home, Search, store/product, account, cart and checkout appearance and behavior. Web currently contains one script for category-image markers and Arabic translation on the website. App currently contains two scripts: it paints the document background purple and drives the native status-bar strip from purple on Home toward white on scroll, while keeping Search purple. Web code does not automatically run in the native app, and App code does not substitute for Web. Shared Global changes can affect both. The 29 September release installed Global and Web together; its follow-up changes only Global and leaves Web/App unchanged. The historical v110 notes describing an App-only Search canvas normalizer and the v111 note describing a measured iOS offset are not the present App source. The present App file has only background and status-strip code. Never infer that the v111 Search correction is active merely from the historical note.

What works
Footer scripts can observe and restyle the actual Vue/Vuetify DOM after it renders. The mobile header was most reliable when styled after render with inline style.setProperty(..., "important") and reapplied after mutations; a static App Custom CSS rule alone was unreliable for the nested header. Real native actions were retained: the new top cart control forwards to the existing cart button or checkout route, product Add stays native, Search uses the native input and results, and account/checkout styles target the native controls. JavaScript can mark category cards with data-hg-category-key and related attributes, permitting scoped category styling without hitting unrelated merchant images. Current source checks parse the scripts and replay real captured markup at phone, tablet and desktop widths in English and Arabic. They are not an installed-device test.

What does not work or is unproven
Web Footer customization does not cover the native iOS screen by itself. Theme Builder/Header CSS, App Custom CSS and App Footer code fought each other when all tried to own the same header. Static deep header rules were overwritten or applied before nested Vue nodes existed. CSS cannot correct Hyperzod's backend search relevance: a search for Food once returned pet merchants. The custom Search UI can improve presentation and submit the native query, but it is not a replacement search engine. A generated list called "Popular searches" was renamed "Search suggestions" because no popularity analytics backs a popularity claim. Checkout payment choices were absent for the inspected signed-in merchant even after choosing an address; code cannot invent configured payment methods. The test account had no past orders and inspected products had no mandatory variant, so those complete native journeys are unverified. Native iOS keyboard, touch gestures, status bar and safe-area behavior still need confirmation in the installed release. Earlier browser fixtures passed while screenshots showed side gutters, so a passing browser-width check must never be reported as proof of a fixed native screen.

Web and app
The website has the desktop AppBar/search field in addition to mobile markup. The app has a native status-bar layer and can wrap the route in a positioned or padded canvas that differs from the website's .v-main shell. iOS also supplied .merchant-mobile-view on shop pages in cases where browser fixtures omitted it; conversely, other mobile shop renderers lacked that class. Therefore all-store product rules must cover both states and both native product renderers. The app's status bar can be driven only within what its WebView/native shell permits; document CSS alone does not prove control of the OS clock/icon color. Hyperzod may keep hidden drawers or old route nodes mounted during transitions, so DOM existence is not the same as the active visible screen. Dialogs and bottom sheets can be teleported outside #app; selectors rooted only at #app miss them. English and Arabic routes and document language can briefly disagree: an /en/ URL was observed with html lang="ar". Arabic direction and text should be checked against the live state, and route links must preserve locale.

Colors and type
Primary brand purple is #5A29DE. The Home/Search mobile header and native status strip use brighter #8640FC. Other active purple shades include #6C35F0, #6C35E8 and #4D22C7 in gradients; the landing page defines #4720B8 and #351482 as darker purple tokens. Orange #FF4000 is the brand accent/cart count; the readable discount-badge variant is #D93600. Green #ACE4AA and lavender #CCCBE3 were used as secondary brand colors. Main dark text is #1B2023; secondary text includes #5C5C5C, #73737C/#767676 and other gray variants. Neutral canvas is often literal #FFFFFF, image tiles use #F5F5F5 or #F7F7F8, and hairline borders use pale gray/lavender such as #E5E5E5, #EAE4F5, #EEECEF and #F0F0F0. An older code comment cites a pure-white/pure-black ban in a brand guide, but the current source explicitly uses #FFFFFF and some #000/#111; treat the current source as the implementation, not that historical comment as a claim of compliance.

The storefront UI generally inherits Hyperzod's existing font rather than forcing a new font throughout. The landing page specifies Inter followed by platform system fonts (SF Pro, Segoe UI, Arial); it imports Noto Sans Arabic for Arabic. Common final mobile headings are 18–22px bold depending on section, with 14–16px product/body text. Search merchant and product preview text is deliberately smaller than shop-page product text. The landing uses custom variables for purple, lavender, dark text, muted text and borders. Boldness and sizes vary by component; the exact current declaration for every selector appears in the selector index below.

Home decisions
The mobile Home header is one flat purple root, with transparent nested toolbar layers so seams do not appear. It has rounded lower corners, white location text, a real search field, the existing filter control restyled, a filled black bag cart icon at the top right, and an orange count badge when needed. The root scrolls with the page rather than remaining permanently sticky. The header's search row has intentional lower purple breathing room, and its placeholder rotates through search prompts. Desktop header rules are separate. The bottom navigation hides its cart item visually once the top cart control exists; the functional native cart remains available to receive clicks. Account and order navigation keep their native routes.

Home categories use a compact square image grid. On mobile, category pages are arranged as swipeable groups of two rows; desktop uses a single wide row. Real category art should be uploaded through the native editor. Generated Home sections reuse real merchants and logo/cover URLs: a purple discovery hero, featured store rail, Brands near you, several merchant-category rails, and remaining stores. Logo tiles use contain/proportional sizing; forcing both image dimensions stretched real merchant marks and produced white framing. Desktop category title accents are centered to the heading, not positioned from the whole category section. Empty delivery time, invalid distance and an orphaned separator are hidden rather than shown as "0" or "• km". Do not swap a merchant's real logo for its cover without considering intended fit.

Search decisions
The current Search opening view has the native purple header, a rounded search field with back and filled black bag/cart, two-row horizontally swipeable category discovery, up to six recent searches from localStorage, Brands near you from real merchant data, and neutral two-row Search suggestion chips. Suggestions are not falsely labeled popularity data. The content canvas is white. Horizontal category/brand/product rails allow native finger gestures and vertical page scrolling while a finger starts on a card; avoid preventDefault or a restrictive touch-action. Selected cards keep their scroll positions and are not rebuilt every polling interval while a finger is touching them. A query hides discovery; clearing it restores discovery. The script automatically submits a native Search query after about 450 ms of idle typing, does not submit a single character or during IME composition, cancels duplicate automatic submission when the user presses Enter, and guards against sending a query after leaving the Search route. It binds inputs once and rebinds when the route remounts. The desktop AppBar input is a separate case. The active Stores tab is only relabeled; its native Vuetify structure is kept. Merchant rows are compact; product groups have horizontally swipeable 136–146px preview tiles, two-line names, aligned prices and restrained View All controls. Search focus is drawn around the rounded pill, not around the square inner input.

The full-bleed iOS Search requirement caused repeated trouble. Fixed negative margins, 100vw roots, wrapper overflow release, a measured-left approach and 16px versus 24px screenshot-scale guesses were tried in successive revisions. Some made the whole page scroll sideways or shifted clipping from one side to the other. The current Global source still contains several historical v104–v108 mobile Search layers, including a width:calc(100vw + 32px) and margin-left:-16px fallback. The current App source does not include the later measured Search correction described in the v111 note. Treat actual right/left edge behavior as device-unverified; measure the real route bounds and identify the clipping ancestor before any future change. Keep intentional 16px content padding inside a truly full-width canvas; "no white side border" never meant zero readable inner padding.

Store and product decisions
Every store must use the same product presentation irrespective of merchant identity, category name, or whether Hyperzod emits .scheme-merchant-product-grid-card or .product-horizontal-cards. The v102 mobile target, based on the MADFOON LAND REST screenshot, is one full-width product row per line: 108 x 108px image at left in English, 16px corners, two-line 16px regular title, one visible 13px description line, 16px medium price at the bottom of the text column, a 34px circular Add control with a 15px plus inset about 4px from the image corner, and a thin divider between rows. Arabic reverses the appropriate side alignment. Recommended Products stays a separate horizontal rail; it is not turned into the single-row category layout. Earlier v98/v101 two-column category tiles and 146px square images were intermediate fixes, not the final all-store mobile category target. Desktop keeps a multi-column tile structure (five columns in store/filtered results) and reserves two title lines, about 41.6px at 16px/1.3, so one- and two-line names place prices on the same baseline. A clear 10px name-to-price gap was chosen after 4px looked cramped. The original category-card border, Vuetify underlay/outline and shadow are removed from product tiles; intentionally thin row dividers remain. White product imagery needs a visible edge around the white Add circle. Product images use consistent square frames; avoid residual percentage padding that makes a 146px image about 292px tall. The product details bottom sheet retains native choices, notes, quantity and Add, with safe-area clearance and readable 16px text inputs. Search, filtered in-store Search and Recommended have their own scopes but use the same control principles.

Account, cart and checkout decisions
Account mobile pages have a purple header/status strip, centered subpage title and a 44px back target. The profile groups native activity/preferences rows; the language choices are card-like and show the real native radio selection. Arabic labels and a few Hyperzod typos are corrected in visible text only. Edit Profile and Address editors are native drawers/bottom sheets and need selectors that work outside #app. The desktop marketing footer is hidden on short mobile account forms. Orders' empty state stays white with a proportional illustration and real Start Shopping action. The cart keeps native product rows, quantity actions, prices and currency precision; buttons are sized with their 108px-wide quantity wrapper so nothing clips. Checkout cards use light borders, readable table totals and genuine primary/disabled states. A disabled Place Order button stays visibly disabled; its color is not made to imply a functioning payment path. Coupon overlay gets a visible bilingual Close control that forwards to the native dismissal, without replacing Apply or changing coupon availability. Safe-area bottom padding protects footer actions. The BHD display supports three-decimal precision rather than rounding away a real platform value.

Landing decisions
The separate landing source retains the original HyperGo logo/artwork and the order Hero → black moving category strip → Featured Stores → Sell With HyperGo → native footer. It uses professional line SVGs rather than emoji in the category ticker, bilingual approved copy, Noto Sans Arabic, purple/lavender surfaces and actual vendor links. The ticker has ten categories and a reduced-motion safeguard; approximate item spacing was 36px desktop and 29px mobile in the prior handoff. Arabic CSS for the landing uses .hg-landing:lang(ar) because :lang inherits the document language and behaved more reliably there than relying only on html[lang^="ar"]. A specific Bahrain-word span .hg-bahrain-tail fixed a visible Arabic joining/spacing issue. The native footer root was identified as #MainFooter / #MultiVendorFooterRoot. The landing is separate from Global/Web/App footers; its present public placement is not verified.

Repeated failures and fixes
1. Duplicating Search/header code across Global, Web, App Custom CSS and Theme Builder made source order misleading and caused conflicting sizing. Ownership was narrowed: Global shared visuals, Web category/translation behavior, App status bar/native shell behavior. Older layers remain in the current Global source, so inspect the cascade before adding another override.
2. A broad .swiper-slide img or [class*="swiper-slide"] img selector hit merchant/store imagery as well as category art. Use the native category editor or data-hg-category-key within the confirmed category section.
3. Two forced logo background dimensions distorted wide/tall logos. Use contain or one automatic axis and preserve source aspect ratio.
4. Styling only .merchant-mobile-view missed mobile variants; styling only #merchant-content missed category sections rendered elsewhere. Scope by the actual .scheme-merchant-page .cat-section and cover both product-card renderers.
5. Vuetify's .v-card border, ::before outline and .v-card__underlay survived a simple border:0. Remove the actual native outline/underlay on the correct card layer, while keeping intended row dividers.
6. iOS inherited an old horizontal-card rule and percentage image padding after a new two-column rule was added; product images became tall and text narrow. Explicitly reset direction, flex sizing, width, height and padding for the active mobile renderer.
7. A CSS zoom of 0.88 made every gap look smaller. It was restored to 1, with actual spacing adjusted where needed.
8. Desktop Search discovery was generated by JavaScript but originally styled only under the mobile media query, leaving raw text on desktop. Desktop-specific discovery rules were added.
9. CSS-only full bleed experiments assumed the app used a .v-main wrapper and later assumed a screenshot pixel equaled one CSS pixel. The iOS route had its own positioned/inset canvas. Several checks passed synthetic wrappers yet native screenshots still showed borders. Do not repeat a blind fixed offset.
10. Re-rendering discovery every 350 ms during a touch reset the rail mid-swipe. Compare data signatures, defer during touch, and preserve scrollLeft.
11. Moving the real cart DOM element into the header risked Vue ownership. A separate top control delegates to the real cart instead.
12. A document-level MutationObserver can create a feedback loop if it rewrites the same text/class/attribute every pass. Compare old and new values, observe only what is necessary, throttle, and clean up route state. A debounced-only update can also starve on a constantly mutating page.
13. A hidden mounted drawer was mistaken for an active page, changing status color and form styles elsewhere. Check visibility and inert ancestors; prefer the visible profile node during transitions.
14. Hyperzod's text translation can reuse a Vue text node for a new label. Store and restore the original carefully, or stale English/Arabic labels overwrite fresh content.
15. Replacing native checkout/search/cart state with decorative markup would hide broken functionality rather than fix it. Keep native handlers and actual configured data.
16. Clearing browser cache or simply publishing a footer is not proof of a visible change. Check the specific route, whether the right Global/Web/App fields loaded, and the installed app after a full restart. The root landing URL may redirect, so its separate source can change without a marketplace page looking different.

Late loading and overrides
Hyperzod uses Vue/Vuetify, so route components, drawers, product cards, language text and category/merchant data arrive or change after footer code initially runs. Some native elements exist hidden and become visible later. Native CSS can be injected after initial render; Vuetify controls have outline, underlay, ripple and overlay sub-elements that a parent rule does not remove. Existing code counters this with scoped high-specificity selectors, !important, late <style> creation, inline setProperty(...,"important"), MutationObserver, resize/scroll listeners, and limited polling. The dynamic Global style IDs are hg-extra-style, hg-home-v88, hg-wm-style, hg-cart-style, hg-ph-style, hg-nav-style, hg-cat-style, hg-acct-style, hg-lang-style, hg-forms-style and hg-rtl-style. Their rules are included in the selector index even though hypergo.css does not contain them. Search additionally has literal style layers hg-v81, hg-search-v97, hg-v99, hg-v100, hg-v101, hg-v102, hg-v103, hg-v104, hg-v105, hg-v106, hg-v108, hg-v109 and hg-customer-experience. Later-looking source order is insufficient when dynamically inserted rules, specificity and !important differ. The app status-bar element .native-status-bar-bg is a native layer reused by the App script; scrim states and overlays must be excluded from normal Home scroll-color logic. The native Search route canvas can have an offset/clipping ancestor outside the selector one initially expects. The Web category script waits for category markup, marks actual image nodes, and tracks language changes. The Search discovery waits for merchant data; the Home rows wait for selected location and API category data. Supplied image URLs may not exist until later, so empty local test photos with network disabled are not evidence that live photos are missing.

Selector index method
The index below was generated from browser-parsed CSS rules in all literal Global <style> tags, all Global JavaScript-created style blocks (including conditional account, forms, Arabic and category styles), and the separate current landing CSS. The current Web and App footer files have no literal CSS rule blocks; App sets inline styles at runtime. A numbered entry shows the exact selector or selector group, its source style ID, any media condition, the controlled area, and every parsed CSS declaration. Comma-separated selector groups are kept together because each member receives the same declarations; no group member is omitted. A class/ID glossary follows the rule index so native and HyperGo-created names can be located quickly. The browser parser ignores CSS comments and may normalize whitespace/quotes; for byte-exact syntax use the authoritative source files. This is the inventory of active source definitions, including rules later superseded in the cascade, not a promise that every selector matches a node on every route. Historical selectors mentioned only in old notes are called out separately after the glossary.

Key selector map
#MultiVendorHeaderRoot is the mobile Home header shell; #MultiVendorHeader and .home-mobile-search-row hold its toolbar and search row. #CurrentLocationBtn is the real changing location control. #MobileSearchFilterBtn is the native filter action. #AppBar and .web-main-search are the desktop search area. #MultiVendorBottomNav, .classic-nav-item and .classic-footer-btn are native bottom navigation, including the underlying cart button. #hg-cart-btn and #hg-search-cart are HyperGo's top bag controls; they forward to that native action. .native-status-bar-bg is the app status strip.
#MultiVendorHome, #ProductCategories, #NearbyMerchants and #hg-cat-rows are the native Home root, categories, old nearby section and generated Home discovery rows. .hg-logo-img and .hg-brand-img are logo artwork tiles; .hg-sec-h and .hg-cat-block are the section heading/marker structure. #MultiVendorSearch is the Search route; .scheme-global-search-mobile-header, .mobile-search-input and .recent-searches are its native header, field and category/recent container. #hg-search-discovery, .hg-sd-brands and .hg-sd-chips are the generated discovery sections and rails. .hg-search-product-rail, .hg-search-product-card and .hg-search-product-group mark native result structures for scoped compact styling.
.scheme-merchant-page and .cat-section identify a store and its product category. .scheme-merchant-product-grid-card and .product-horizontal-cards are the two different native category-product renderers. .scheme-product-recommendation-section is the separate recommendation rail. .pro-card-h-img and .product-image-frame are image frames; .product-name, .product-description, .price and .add-btn are the product text, price and native Add control. #MerchantSearchPage identifies filtered in-store Search. .product-popup.product-model is the native product bottom sheet. .scheme-filter-panel is its native filter UI. #ProfileSideBar, #Languages, #addresses and #custom-scroll-orders are native account, language, address and orders areas. .scheme-cart-panel, #cartItems2, #checkout, #OrderPlaceButton and .scheme-coupon-content identify the native cart, checkout and coupon controls. .hg-landing scopes the separate landing page. Every more specific selector, state and exact declaration is indexed below.

Animations
The CSS rule index covers style selectors, not @keyframes. Global runtime CSS defines hgGoo for the pull-to-refresh graphic. The language-switch veil creates hgspin, a 0.8-second rotating loading ring, inside its own runtime <style> tag; it appears only when that veil is mounted. The landing CSS defines hg-hero-float, hg-category-scroll and hg-logo-scroll-right for gentle hero and ticker/logo motion, with reduced-motion handling in the landing source. App footer status-color animation is computed in JavaScript while scrolling rather than declared as a CSS keyframe.

RUNTIME ATTRIBUTES AND NON-CSS SELECTORS
The scripts also use DOM queries and data hooks. These are behavior anchors even when they have no independent CSS rule. data-hg-category-key identifies the real category (food, retail, flowers, beauty, electronics, perfumes, fashion, pharmacy, nutrition, pets, grocery, home-goods, sports, toys, gift-shop, baby-care or party); data-hg-category-image and data-hg-category-image-wrapper identify the marked image and frame. data-hg-nav identifies native nav destinations; data-hg-keyboard marks an element given Enter/Space keyboard activation. data-q stores a Search suggestion. data-sig/data-v avoid unnecessary discovery/category rerendering; data-touching/data-gestures protect an active swipe. data-hgc records already-cleaned merchant metadata. The actual JS query and class operations remain in the three footer sources; the CSS index is complete for rule selectors, while DOM-query strings can include dynamic expressions and are not equivalent to style rules.

HISTORICAL SELECTORS OUTSIDE THE CURRENT FOOTER INVENTORY
The older handoff identified #MultiVendorHeader, #CurrentLocationBtn, #MobileSearchFilterBtn, .home-mobile-search-row, .home-mobile-search-input, .v-input__control, .v-field, .v-field__input, .v-field__append-inner and .v-field__prepend-inner in the rendered header; #MultiVendorSearch, .scheme-global-search-mobile-header, .mobile-search-input and .recent-searches on Search; #MultiVendorBottomNav, .classic-nav-item, button.classic-footer-btn, .classic-footer-btn__content and .nav-label in bottom navigation; and #MainFooter / #MultiVendorFooterRoot in the native footer. Those names are observed DOM hooks even where not every one appears in a current CSS rule. The old proposed broad .swiper-slide img and [class*="swiper-slide"] img selectors were abandoned because they also hit store imagery. The handoff describes a separate Theme Builder category-image rule set using img[data-hg-category-key="..."] and [data-hg-category-image-wrapper="1"], but that exact current admin CSS was not available in the active workspace and is not asserted as installed.

Its exact category-image keys were img[data-hg-category-key="food"], "retail", "flowers", "beauty", "electronics", "perfumes", "fashion", "pharmacy", "nutrition", "pets", "grocery", "home-goods", "sports", "toys", "gift-shop", "baby-care" and "party". Each targets only the corresponding marked native category image. [data-hg-category-image-wrapper="1"] and its ::after pseudo-element provide the image frame/overlay; img[data-hg-category-image="1"] and its hover variants control the marked image presentation. .hg-search-icon and .hg-landing:lang(ar) .hg-bahrain-tail are additional historical custom hooks for the header icon and a targeted Arabic word-spacing fix; the latter remains in the separate landing CSS.

EVIDENCE AND LIMITS
Current source and history: the five authoritative files named at the top; REFERENCE/HyperGo_Claude_Full_Handoff.md; REFERENCE/v97_Search_changes.md through v111_ios_search_edges.md; REFERENCE/CUSTOMER_EXPERIENCE_CHECKLIST.md; and RELEASES/CUSTOMER_FOLLOWUP_2026-09-29/INSTALL.md. The exact rule inventory was generated by Chromium CSSOM from these current source files, including runtime-created style nodes in a local fixture. It verifies syntax and inventory, not live matching, the final computed cascade on every route, or installed-device rendering. No undocumented box limit, publication result or backend behavior has been inferred.
